Temporal spike: whether a Windows Job Object's members can be found and ended after its supervisor dies
<!-- ABOUTME: Spike card: whether a later process can find and end the members of a Windows Job Object after the supervisor that created it has died. -->
- **Kind:** `spike`. Maker `spikeEngineer` (`rigger.config.mjs`, `kinds.spike`). Its code is throwaway and never merges (`AGENTS.md`, "When you write code").
- **Dispatch:** by hand, by the rigger-ae coordinator. It runs beside M8 and touches nothing of M8's; no M8 card waits for it.
- **Branch:** `rigger-<card>`, for the report's docs-only pull request.
- **Lands in:**
  - `docs/spikes/<report>.md`, the report.
  - `docs/spikes/<report>/`, the evidence the report cites.

  - `doc-references.json`, the report's entry.
  - `spikes/`, gitignored: the throwaway work, never committed.
- **Attacked:** pending: pre-dispatch attack.
- **Authorized:** the owner, in an interactive session on 2026-11-21, ruling the four epics, relayed by rigger-ae at [1453#7072953923](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1453#issuecomment-7072953923).

Part of the **Temporal** track, epic #1454 (whole-tree containment). **Author:** the PM.

**The track.** The Temporal track is a post-v0 exploration; its record is #1453. Three spikes (#1429, #1430, #1431) measured what Temporal gives a card's dispatch and what it does not. #1453 recommends going ahead after v0 on condition, in this order: whole-tree containment on every OS (E1), a design that stops a second attempt running alongside the first (E2), and only then the Temporal runtime (E3) and the board as a mirror (E4). The owner ruled the four epics on 2026-11-21 ([1453#7072953923](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1453#issuecomment-7072953923)). Cards are filed just in time; this one is in its epic's first wave. Nothing here records a decision, amends a requirement or changes `ARCHITECTURE.md` or `README.md`; a finding that calls for one is a proposal for the PM, the architect and the owner.

---

**The question.** After the supervisor that created a dispatch's Job Object dies, can a process started later find the job, or every one of its members, and end them all?

**Why a spike.** #1430 found that without kill-on-job-close every member outlived a supervisor killed with `taskkill /F`, and that a fresh process could not then open the job: `OpenJobObjectW` returned `ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND`, because the job's name went with the supervisor's handle, the last one open (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment.md`, "E2 without kill-on-job-close (x4)"). Its report says the "next start ends the remains" design that `R-STATE-10` has does not carry over to Windows without a second handle holder, which was not measured. It also names resume across a supervisor restart, the Temporal track's aim, as something that may need dispatches to outlive the supervisor, which kill-on-job-close forbids ("Native Windows: a Job Object per dispatch, with kill-on-job-close"). Whether any route keeps an orphaned job's members reachable is a property of the host.

**What exists already, as context that earns no credit.**

- #1430's Windows fixture and supervisor: `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture-win/`, and its members M1 to M4 and M6 ("The fixture"). The spike may reuse them, and says if it changed them.
- #1430's x3 and x4: with kill-on-job-close set, killing the supervisor ended every member; without it, every member lived on and the job could not be opened by name ("The table").
- Sibling card #1458 asks whether a non-elevated supervisor can use Job Objects at all. This card does not wait for it.

**The setup.**

- **Host:** the Windows machine (`hostpc`).
- **The routes it weighs.** At least the second handle holder that #1430 names: a process other than the supervisor that holds a handle to the dispatch's job and outlives the supervisor. The spike weighs any other route it finds that could let a later process reach the job or its members, and says why it ran or did not run each.
- **The cases,** for each route it runs:
  - **K1:** the supervisor is killed outright while the tree runs, and every other process the route relies on stays alive. Then a fresh process, started after the kill, tries to end every member;
  - **K2:** the supervisor and every other process the route relies on are killed outright while the tree runs, each with `taskkill /F` or `Stop-Process -Force`, and the transcript records each kill. Then a fresh process tries to end every member;
  - each case once with kill-on-job-close set on the job and once without it.
- **If no route lets a fresh process end every member,** the card is still done: that is the answer, and the report names the call that failed in each route.
- **The integrity level** each run had is the spike's choice, given what is available, and the report says what its choice leaves unproved.
- **Survival** is read at least 10 s after an ending, the bound #1430 used.

**Scope edges.** A host reboot, a breakaway flag, a real agent session, and the elevated E1 and E2 results #1430 measured are outside this card.

**Hosts, and whose custody each is under.** #1429's section "Hosts, and whose custody each is under" binds this card as written there, except for the Mac mini's rule, which is below: the WSL2 machine and the Windows machine as the primary hosts and how each is reached, and the laptop's list of what it never takes. #1430's report found that `wsl-hostpc` did not answer and reached WSL through `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e …` (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment.md`, "What ran, and where"). Any use of the Mac mini goes through the rigger-d1 coordinator first: the spike asks rigger-ae on this card, waits for rigger-ae to relay rigger-d1's OK, and runs every command there through the mini's suite-slot script (`tools/coordinator/suite-slot.sh` in this repository), at the path and with the environment rigger-d1's terms give at dispatch, so that it takes the same slot M8's work does.

**Rules on how it runs.** #1429's "Rules on how it runs" binds this card as written there, with this card's number in place of #1429's, except its "Sibling spikes share the WSL2 machine" rule, which the "Siblings share hosts" bullet below replaces. Three of #1429's rules cost the last three spikes several judge rounds, so this card tightens them:

- **Exact commands.** The transcript holds every command exactly as it ran, character for character, with its UTC time and host. A command written up from memory, or condensed, is not a transcript entry. A timed figure names the transcript entries of the commands that produced both its endpoints.
- **Failed runs in full.** A run that failed, was abandoned or was repeated keeps its full, unabridged output under `docs/spikes/<report>/`. The report says why each one failed or was abandoned.
- **Every pid, recorded at its start.** The processes that count are the supervisor, every fixture member, and every other process still running when the command that started it returned, on any host and under any account, every handle holder or other process a route relies on included. A process that a spike process spawns may be recorded by the process that spawns it, at the moment it spawns it. A command that exits before it returns is covered by its transcript entry, and needs no record entry.
  - Each entry in the process record, under `docs/spikes/<report>/`, carries the process's host, its pid, its creation time as the host reports it, and the UTC time the entry was written. The entry is written as the process starts and before the next command on that host.
  - A process is the recorded one only where both its pid and its creation time match an entry; a pid alone is not a match, because Windows reuses pids quickly.
  - A pid found afterwards in a listing is a disclosure, not a record. The spike ends only recorded processes and their descendants (`AGENTS.md`, "End only recorded processes").
- **Siblings share hosts.** The sibling set is #1458, #1459, #1460, #1462 and #1463, and any of them may touch the WSL2 machine or the Windows machine while this card runs. Before a WSL restart, a `.wslconfig` or `/etc/wsl.conf` change, a logout of a user's sessions, or any other step that ends processes it did not start, the spike posts the step on each other card in that set and waits for each one's spike, or rigger-ae, to answer that none of its recorded processes would be ended.

## Acceptance

- The report gives the Windows build, the Node version and the version of each package the supervisor loaded, each quoted from the command that printed it.
- The report names every route it weighed, and states why it ran or did not run each.
- For each run, the report quotes the mandatory label of the supervisor process's own token, read from that process during that run.
- The report names what the integrity levels it ran at leave unproved about the other levels.
- For each route, case and kill-on-job-close setting it ran, the report quotes a listing taken before the kill that shows, for each fixture member, whether it is in the dispatch's job.
- For each route, case and kill-on-job-close setting it ran, the report quotes a listing taken after the kill and before the fresh process acted that shows, for each fixture member, whether it is alive.
- For each route, case and kill-on-job-close setting it ran, the report quotes the calls the fresh process made to reach the job or its members, each with its return value and, where it failed, its error code.
- For each route, case and kill-on-job-close setting it ran, the report quotes a listing taken at least 10 s after the fresh process acted that shows, for each fixture member, whether it is alive.
- For each route, case and kill-on-job-close setting where the fresh process ended members, the report states how the fresh process established that the job or processes it ended were the dispatch's, and not a job or process that reused a name or pid.
- For each route, case and kill-on-job-close setting it ran, the report states, citing the transcript, whether the session that started the supervisor was still open when each post-kill listing was taken.
- For each route, case and kill-on-job-close setting it did not run, the report states why.
- For each route and each process the route relies on, the report quotes which job, if any, that process ran in.
- Each run the report counts toward its recommendation had the session that started the supervisor, and the session of every process the route relies on, open until the last post-kill listing, as the transcript shows; the report names any other run as unattributed.
- Where no route let a fresh process end every member, the report says so as its answer, naming the call that failed in each route.
- The report recommends whether, and by which route, a process started after a supervisor's death can end every member of that supervisor's job, on the evidence above.
- The report states which of its routes, if any, lets a dispatch's tree outlive its supervisor and still be ended later.
- The report says what evidence would reverse its recommendation.

- Every command the report quotes appears in the transcript character for character as it ran.
- Every figure the report marks as measured names the transcript entries of the commands that produced its endpoints.
- Every figure the report gives that is not measured is marked as judgment, with its premise.
- For every run that failed, was abandoned or was repeated, the report gives the path of that run's full, unabridged output under `docs/spikes/<report>/`, and says why it failed or was abandoned.
- The report gives the path of a process record under `docs/spikes/<report>/` listing every process that counts under "Every pid, recorded at its start", each with its host, pid, creation time and the UTC time it was recorded.
- Each entry in the process record carries a recorded time earlier than the transcript's next command on that host after the process started.
- For each host the spike used, the report quotes a process listing taken after its last run, showing each process's pid and creation time, in which no process matches a record entry on both.

---- williacj 2026-11-22T02:48:03Z
**Pre-dispatch attack** (architect, under `.claude/skills/acceptance/` "Have it attacked before dispatch": items depend on a system outside Rigger's code). Every citation was opened on `origin/main` at 280ec1bf. The card asks for exact timed commands, full output for failed runs, and every pid recorded at its start. Its items 15–22 under "Acceptance" make each of those ruleable. The findings follow.

1. **The kill-on-job-close axis is in the setup but not in the items.** "The setup" runs each case "once with kill-on-job-close set on the job and once without it". Every item says only "for each route and case it ran", so a run with one setting per case meets them all. **Fix:** in items 5–11, write "for each route, case and kill-on-job-close setting".
2. **A member can be ended by a job that is not the dispatch's.** #1430 found the SSH session's own job ended every member on disconnect ("Was Node already in a job"). The second handle holder may also sit in some session's job. Item 10 asks whether the session was open, but nothing stops a run with the session closed from counting toward item 12. **Fix:** add "Each run the report counts toward its recommendation had the session that started the supervisor, and the session of every process the route relies on, open until the last post-kill listing, as the transcript shows." Also add an item: for each process the route relies on, the report quotes which job, if any, it ran in.
3. **K2 does not say how the route's other processes are killed.** "Killed outright" is defined for the supervisor (#1430's E2) but not for the handle holder. **Fix:** say that K2 ends each process the route relies on with `taskkill /F` or `Stop-Process -Force`, and that the transcript records each one.
4. **Two sibling rules bind at once.** "Rules on how it runs" binds #1429's rules "as written there, with this card's number in place of #1429's". That carries in #1429's "Sibling spikes share the WSL2 machine", which says to post on "all three cards", now the closed #1429–#1431. This card's own "Siblings share hosts" bullet says to post on "each sibling card" and names only "the E1 spikes". A maker cannot tell which set to wait for. **Fix:** say this card's bullet replaces #1429's rule, and name the sibling set as #1458, #1459, #1460, #1462 and #1463. All five may touch the WSL2 or Windows machine.
5. **"Every process it starts" has no stated scope.** It is unclear whether it covers the fixture members the fixture spawns, the supervisor, one-shot commands such as a listing or `ssh`, and processes run under another account. Each reading gives a different process record, and so a different ruling on "Each entry in the process record carries a recorded time earlier than…". **Fix:** define it as every process still running when the command that started it returned, plus the supervisor and every fixture member. A process that a spike process spawns may be recorded by the process that spawns it, at the moment it spawns it. A command that exits before returning is covered by its transcript entry. The handle holder is a process this card adds, and it needs an entry of its own.
6. **The final sweep can pass on a reused pid, or fail on one.** "a process listing taken after its last run in which none of the recorded pids is alive" reads pids alone, and Windows reuses them quickly. **Fix:** record each process's creation time beside its pid, and have the sweep match on both. This card's item 9, on telling the dispatch's processes from processes that reuse a name or pid, already asks the same of the fresh process. The sweep should meet the same bar.

ABOUTME: Spike findings for card #1430: whether cgroup v2 under WSL2 and Job Objects on Windows end an
agent's whole process tree, measured against the macOS baseline and today's route.

# Whole-tree containment on Linux under WSL2 and on Windows

## The answer

On Linux under WSL2, the answer is yes. An unprivileged supervisor obtained a cgroup it could write
to from the systemd user manager, with no root. Writing cgroup.kill ended all six fixture
members, including the two that left the session and the one that moved to `/`. The cgroup reported
no live process between 0.88 and 1.66 ms after the write, in the three runs that measured it. When the supervisor was
killed outright, all six members lived on in the orphaned cgroup. A fresh process that wrote
cgroup.kill to that cgroup ended every one of them.

On native Windows, the answer is also yes, with one condition. A Node supervisor reached Job Objects
through koffi, an FFI package with a prebuilt binary. Every member it started, M1 to M4 and M6, was
in its job. `TerminateJobObject` ended all of them. With kill-on-job-close set, killing the
supervisor with `taskkill /F` ended every member too. Without kill-on-job-close, every member
outlived the supervisor. A fresh process could not then reach the job, because its name disappeared
with the supervisor's last handle. The condition is that this supervisor ran elevated (High
Mandatory Level). A non-elevated supervisor was not measured.

Today's route (`R-STATE-17`) left M6 alive on both macOS and Linux. M6 is the member that starts a
new session and changes its working directory to `/`. The group kill and the working-directory
census found every other member. On macOS, the process-group kill alone also left M3 alive, the
member that starts a new session. Killing the supervisor outright left every member alive on macOS,
as `R-STATE-9` and `R-STATE-10` expect.

Round 2 repeated every measurement on every host with every process the runs started recorded by
...

10	members, including the two that left the session and the one that moved to `/`. The cgroup reported
11	no live process between 0.88 and 1.66 ms after the write, in the three runs that measured it. When the supervisor was
12	killed outright, all six members lived on in the orphaned cgroup. A fresh process that wrote
13	cgroup.kill to that cgroup ended every one of them.
14	
15	On native Windows, the answer is also yes, with one condition. A Node supervisor reached Job Objects
16	through koffi, an FFI package with a prebuilt binary. Every member it started, M1 to M4 and M6, was
17	in its job. `TerminateJobObject` ended all of them. With kill-on-job-close set, killing the
18	supervisor with `taskkill /F` ended every member too. Without kill-on-job-close, every member
19	outlived the supervisor. A fresh process could not then reach the job, because its name disappeared
20	with the supervisor's last handle. The condition is that this supervisor ran elevated (High
21	Mandatory Level). A non-elevated supervisor was not measured.

28

31	("Round 2", below).
32	
33	I recommend a delegated cgroup ended by cgroup.kill for Linux under WSL2, and a Job Object with
34	kill-on-job-close for native Windows. Both are under "Recommendations", with what would reverse
35	each. This report hands over evidence. It changes no code, requirement or recorded decision.

53	| wr1, wr2, wr3 | WSL2 | round 2: E1, E1c, E2 with the fresh writer | orch.cjs ran `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e bash -s fx1430r2` with a script on stdin. The script execs into the driver in a transient `systemd-run --user --scope` unit, and the driver starts each supervisor as a transient systemd user service, as round 1 did |
54	| xr1, xr2, xr3 | Windows | round 2: E1, E2 with kill-on-job-close, E2 without it and the fresh reach | orch.cjs ran `ssh hostpc` with `node driver-win.cjs`. That started each supervisor detached |
55	
56	WSL was reached only through `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e …`, because `wsl-hostpc` did not answer. In every
57	WSL run, the process that launched the tree, the Node supervisor, lived inside WSL. It was a child
58	of the user's systemd manager (pid 339), as each listing's parent pid column shows. It was never a
59	Windows process.
60	
61	`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/` holds the evidence:
62	
63	- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/transcript.txt`, round 1's commands run through its wrapper.
64	- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/`, the fixture source for macOS and Linux.
65	- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture-win/`, the fixture source for Windows.
66	- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/runners/`, the scripts that drove the round-1 runs.
67	- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/`, round 2's code, its transcript and its process records.
68	- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/session-log.txt`, the tool calls in the spike engineer's
69	  retained session record, with the gaps "The transcript" lists.
70	- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/ci-fast-136023691075-excerpt.txt`, the failure output of the
71	  CI run that failed on this pull request.

87	## The fixture
88	
89	The fixture is one process tree started in a dispatch directory the spike made: `mac/d-<run>` in
90	the laptop's spike directory, `~/1430-spike-engineer/d-<run>` in WSL, and
91	`C:\Users\user\1430-spike-engineer\d-<run>` on Windows. Each member sleeps 300 s unless ended. On
92	macOS and Linux, `argv[0]` is `fx-<run>-M<n>`. On Windows, the command line carries
93	`fx1430-<run>-M<n>`.
94	
95	### How each member was started
96	
97	| Member | macOS and Linux (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/fixture.sh`, run by `bash` as the supervised command) | Windows (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture-win/fixture-win.cjs`, run by `node` as the supervised command) |
98	|---|---|---|
99	| M1 | `bash -c "(exec -a fx-R-M2 sleep 300) & exec -a fx-R-M1 sleep 300" &`: a direct child of the supervised command | `spawn(node, [member-win.cjs, …-M1, 'M1'])`: a direct child |
100	| M2 | the subshell M1 forks before it execs, a grandchild whose parent is M1 | M1 runs `spawn(node, [member-win.cjs, …-M2, 'leaf'])`, a grandchild whose parent is M1 |
101	| M3 | `perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid(); exec {"sleep"} "fx-R-M3", "300"' &`: a new session | `spawn(node, […-M3], { detached: true })`. libuv's src/win/process.c (read at `v1.51.0`) makes this `DETACHED_PROCESS \| CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP`, a new process group |
102	| M4 | `bash -c "(exec -a fx-R-M4 sleep 300) & exit 0" &`: its parent exits at once, so it is reparented | `cmd.exe /d /c start "" /b node member-win.cjs …-M4 leaf`. Its parent cmd.exe exits at once |
103	| M5 | `bash -c "trap '' TERM; exec -a fx-R-M5 sleep 300" &`: `SIGTERM` is ignored, and `SIG_IGN` survives `exec` | not applicable: `TerminateProcess` cannot be ignored |
104	| M6 | `perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid(); chdir "/"; exec {"sleep"} "fx-R-M6", "300"' &`: a new session working in `/` | `spawn(node, […-M6, 'M6'], { detached: true })`. The member runs `process.chdir()` to the drive root, `C:\` |
105	
106	The supervised command waits on stdin for one line before it starts any member. The supervisor moves
107	it into the cgroup or job first, and then writes the line. That closes the race between spawning
108	and assigning. Every supervisor spawned the supervised command with `detached: true`, as
109	`runCommand` in `src/substrate/process.mjs` does, so that command leads a process group of its own.
110	
111	### The supervisors
112	
113	- **macOS and Linux:** `fixture/supervisor.cjs <e1|e1c|e2> <run> <dispatch> <fixture-dir>`.
114	  - On Linux, it reads its own cgroup from `/proc/self/cgroup` and creates a child cgroup `tree`
115	    under it. It writes the supervised command's pid into tree/cgroup.procs.
116	  - E1 on Linux writes `1` to tree/cgroup.kill. It then polls tree/cgroup.events every 1 ms
117	    until it reads `populated 0`.
118	  - E1 on macOS sends `SIGKILL` to the process group.
119	  - E1c, on both, sends `SIGKILL` to the process group. It then runs
120	    `lsof -w -n -P -a -d cwd -u <uid> -F pun`, the argument list `listing` in
121	    `src/substrate/process.mjs` builds, and sends `SIGKILL` to every pid whose working directory
122	    lies under the dispatch directory's real path.
123	  - It lists 3 s after the start and 12 s after the ending.
124	- **Windows:** `fixture-win/supervisor-win.cjs <e1|e2k|e2n> fx1430-<run>-sup <dispatch>`.
125	  - It creates a job named `Local\fx1430-<run>` with `CreateJobObjectW`. For e1 and e2k, it sets
126	    `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` with `SetInformationJobObject(JobObjectExtendedLimitInformation)`.
127	    For e2n it leaves the limit unset.
128	  - It assigns the supervised command with `AssignProcessToJobObject`.
129	  - E1 calls `TerminateJobObject`. It then polls `JobObjectBasicAccountingInformation.ActiveProcesses`
130	    until the count is 0.
131	  - It lists 5 s after the start and 12 s after the ending.
132	- **The listings:**
133	  - `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/list.sh` uses `ps` for pid, parent pid, group and state, and os.getsid for the
134	    session. It takes the working directory from `/proc/<pid>/cwd` on Linux and from `lsof -d cwd`
135	    on macOS, and the cgroup from `/proc/<pid>/cgroup` on Linux.
136	  - `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture-win/win32.cjs` uses `Win32_Process` for pid, parent pid and session. It calls
137	    `IsProcessInJob` against the spike's job and against any job. It reads the working directory
138	    from the process's PEB (`RTL_USER_PROCESS_PARAMETERS.CurrentDirectory`).
139	
140	## Linux under WSL2
141	
142	### The user, cgroup v2, systemd and cgroup.kill
143	
144	**The user (w7, transcript entry 01:01:51Z).** The unit's `ExecStart` ran `id`, and then `exec`ed into the Node
145	supervisor in the same process. It printed:
146	
147	```
148	uid=1000(user) gid=1000(user) groups=1000(user),4(adm),24(cdrom),27(sudo),30(dip),46(plugdev),100(users)
149	```
150	
151	Runs w1 to w6 started the supervisor the same way, without the `id` prefix. Their runner printed the
152	same `id` line. The supervisor ran as the unprivileged user `user`, not root. The user is in `sudo`,
153	but nothing used it.
154	
155	**cgroup v2 is mounted, and the unified hierarchy is in use (00:41:17Z).** `mount | grep cgroup`
156	printed one line, a `cgroup2` mount at `/sys/fs/cgroup` and no v1 hierarchy:
157	
158	```
159	cgroup2 on /sys/fs/cgroup type cgroup2 (rw,nosuid,nodev,noexec,relatime,nsdelegate)
160	```
161	
162	`cat /sys/fs/cgroup/cgroup.controllers` printed `cpuset cpu io memory hugetlb pids rdma`. At
163	00:41:04Z, `stat -fc %T /sys/fs/cgroup` printed `cgroup2fs`.
164	
165	**systemd runs as pid 1 (00:41:04Z).** `ps -p 1 -o pid,comm` printed:
166	
167	```
168	    PID COMMAND
169	      1 systemd
170	```
171	
172	At 00:41:17Z, `systemctl is-system-running` printed `running`, and `systemctl --user is-system-running`
173	also printed `running`. `loginctl show-user user` showed `Service=user@1000.service` and `State=active`.
174	
175	**cgroup.kill exists (00:41:17Z).** `ls /sys/fs/cgroup` listed cgroup.kill at the root.
176	`ls -l /sys/fs/cgroup/non-systemd` showed `--w------- 1 root root 0 Nov 19 18:41 cgroup.kill`. The
177	delegated cgroup in the next item holds a cgroup.kill owned by `user`.
178	
179	**An unprivileged user can obtain a writable cgroup subtree, through the systemd user manager
180	(00:42:55Z).** Two routes worked, and the runs used the second:
181	
182	```
183	$ systemd-run --user --scope -p Delegate=yes cat /proc/self/cgroup
184	Running as unit: run-p511-i2417.scope; invocation ID: f03feebebecb4105b3116a22f05c16e8
185	0::/user.slice/user-1000.slice/user@1000.service/app.slice/run-p511-i2417.scope
186	$ systemd-run --user --wait --pipe -q -p Delegate=yes bash -c "cat /proc/self/cgroup; c=$(cut -d: -f3 /proc/self/cgroup); ls -l /sys/fs/cgroup$c | grep -E \"procs|kill|subtree\"; id"
187	0::/user.slice/user-1000.slice/user@1000.service/app.slice/run-p512-i2418.service
188	--w------- 1 user user 0 Nov 19 18:42 cgroup.kill
189	-rw-r--r-- 1 user user 0 Nov 19 18:42 cgroup.procs
190	-rw-r--r-- 1 user user 0 Nov 19 18:42 cgroup.subtree_control
191	uid=1000(user) gid=1000(user) groups=1000(user),4(adm),24(cdrom),27(sudo),30(dip),46(plugdev),100(users)
192	```
193	
194	The shell that `wsl.exe -e` starts is not in systemd's tree. Its `/proc/self/cgroup` read
195	`0::/non-systemd` at 00:41:04Z, a root-owned cgroup it cannot write to. The scope route moved even
196	that process into a unit of the user manager. The service route starts the process inside the
197	unit. Neither needed root, so the root route was not measured.

321	Every remaining member had ended. The first attempt, w3, gave the same before and after readings.
322	Its listing at 00:51:55Z read `count=0`.
323	
324	### A supplementary reading: E2 under systemd's default `KillMode` (w6)
325	
326	This run is outside the card's endings, because systemd acts in it. Under the default
327	`KillMode=control-group`, systemd sent `SIGTERM` to the unit's cgroup when the supervisor died.
328	Twelve seconds after `kill -9 103945`:
329	
330	```
331	listing run=w6 at 2026-11-20T00:52:58Z on Linux
332	PID     PPID    PGID    SID     STAT  CWD | CGROUP | ARGS
333	103959  339     103955  103955  S     /home/user/1430-spike-engineer/d-w6 | /user.slice/user-1000.slice/user@1000.service/app.slice/fx1430-w6.service/tree | fx-w6-M5 300
334	count=1
335	```
336	
337	Only M5, which ignores `SIGTERM`, was alive, and the unit was still in `stop-sigterm`. The fresh
338	cgroup.kill writer then ended it, and the listing 10 s later read `count=0`. A unit used as a
339	dispatch's container therefore needs `KillSignal=SIGKILL`, or an explicit cgroup.kill, to close M5.

347	transcript, with one account line redacted:

350	BUILTIN\Administrators                                        Alias            S-1-5-32-544   Mandatory group, Enabled by default, Enabled group, Group owner
351	NT AUTHORITY\Local account and member of Administrators group Well-known group S-1-5-114      Mandatory group, Enabled by default, Enabled group
352	Mandatory Label\High Mandatory Level                          Label            S-1-16-12288

357	00:58:20Z with `The system cannot find the path specified.`, so no non-elevated run was made.
358	
359	### Routes from Node to a Job Object
360	
361	| Route | What it installs or builds | Weighed |
362	|---|---|---|
363	| **koffi** (FFI), used | `npm install koffi` added 2 packages in the spike's directory: `koffi@3.3.8` and the prebuilt @koromix/koffi-win32-x64. Nothing was compiled. npm 11.17.0 did not run koffi's install script, which was not yet covered by `allowScripts`, and the prebuilt binary loaded without it | Calls `kernel32` and `ntdll` directly from the supervisor's own process, so the job handle lives and dies with the supervisor. That is the property E2 measures. Adds a native dependency per platform |
364	| A node-gyp native addon | Visual Studio Build Tools with the C++ workload, and Python, on every host that builds it. It builds a .node binary | The same in-process handle as koffi, at the cost of a compiler toolchain on the host. Not built |
365	| A PowerShell or C# helper process (`Add-Type` P/Invoke) spawned by Node | Nothing: PowerShell ships with Windows | The job handle lives in the helper, not the supervisor, so killing the supervisor tests the helper's lifetime instead. Every call also pays a PowerShell start. Not built |
366	
367	### Was Node already in a job (00:56:46Z)
368	
369	Before it created a job, the supervisor called `IsProcessInJob(GetCurrentProcess(), NULL, &r)`:
370	
371	```

373	```
374	
375	The SSH session's processes already sat in a job. After x4, with the session closed, the members x4
376	left had ended. That is evidence that the session's job ends its members on disconnect. Every
377	reading below was taken inside one SSH session, before it closed.

556	
557	## Round 2: every measurement repeated, with every process recorded
558	
559	The round-1 judge found that round 1 recorded only the long-lived processes. It never recorded the
560	pids of short-lived helpers, or of the intermediate process that M4's parent is on macOS and Linux
561	("Every process the spike started", below). Those pids cannot be recovered, so round 2 repeated
562	every card measurement, on every host, on 2026-11-20, with every process the runs started
563	recorded. The stated intent for the new steps went up first, at
564	[1430#7044905230](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1430#issuecomment-7044905230).
565	
566	### How each process was recorded
567	
568	| Host | What recorded it |
569	|---|---|
570	| All | Every Node process loads `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/rec.cjs`. It writes the process's own pid and parent, and the pid, command and exit of every process it starts, as JSON lines. The laptop's step runner, `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/orch.cjs`, records each `ssh` and local command the same way, and writes `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/transcript-r2.txt`: each step's exact argv, its stdin script verbatim with sha256, its UTC start and end, and its full output |
571	| macOS and Linux | `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/fixture.sh` writes the pid of every process it starts, M4's parent included, using bash builtins only. The listing (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/list.cjs`) runs in Node, with one recorded `ps`. On macOS it adds one recorded `lsof -O` and one recorded `python3`, run by its real path rather than through the `/usr/bin/python3` stub |
572	| WSL | Each script fed to `bash -s` writes its own pid and its two WSL init parents, and runs every external command through a `rec` function that prints the command's pid, using bash builtins only. Each supervisor's unit prints the pid of its `id` and of its own bash, which then execs Node |
573	| Windows | A watcher, `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/watcher.ps1`, ran in its own SSH session from 16:34:25Z to 16:41:43Z. It logged every process start and stop on the machine through WMI's `Win32_ProcessStartTrace` and `Win32_ProcessStopTrace`. `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/collect-win.cjs` then picks out the spike's own processes: the watcher's session chain, every per-connection `sshd` that started within 3 s of one of the laptop's recorded `ssh` spawns, and every descendant of either. The result is `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/win-mine-full.json`. The log holds 12 new per-connection `sshd` processes, from 16:35:03Z to 16:41:19Z, and each one matched one of the spike's 12 `ssh` spawns in that span. No other connection arrived while the watcher ran. Every pid that `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/rec.cjs` recorded on Windows is in that set |
574	
575	On Windows every file sat in one directory, so the sources under
576	`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/win/` loaded a byte-identical copy of
577	`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/rec.cjs` beside them. `cmp` confirmed the two were
578	identical, and the copy is not committed twice.
579	
580	Round 2 differs from round 1 only in how it records:
581	
582	- The census and the macOS listing call `lsof -O`, so lsof does its kernel reads in its own
583	  process instead of forked helpers. The census reads the same fields as before.
584	- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/fixture.sh` ends with `exec sleep 300` instead of `sleep 300`, so its tail makes no new process.
585	- The listing runs in Node instead of `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/list.sh`.
586	- WSL was reached only through `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e`, as in round 1. The WSL machine booted again
587	  between sessions, because WSL stops when idle. The final check printed a boot time of 16:41:18Z,
588	  and its pids began again near 400.
589	
590	### What round 2 measured
591	
592	Every cell of the table below matches round 1. Times are in `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/transcript-r2.txt`.
593	
594	| Run | Ending | Reading at least 10 s after the ending |
595	|---|---|---|
596	| mr1, macOS | E1, process-group kill of 74511 at 16:33:16.190Z | count=3: the supervisor, M3 (74528) and M6 (74532) alive. M1, M2, M4 and M5 ended |
597	| mr2, macOS | E1c, group kill of 99236, then the census killed 99254 (M3) | count=2: the supervisor and M6 (99267) alive. M1 to M5 ended |
598	| mr3, macOS | E2, SIGKILL of supervisor 21189 at 16:33:49.272Z | count=7: the supervised command and M1 to M6 all alive |
599	| wr1, WSL2 | E1, cgroup.kill, `populated 0` after 0.88 ms | count=1: the supervisor only. M1 to M6 ended |
600	| wr2, WSL2 | E1c, group kill of 651, then the census killed 653 (M3) | count=2: the supervisor and M6 (656) alive. M1 to M5 ended |
601	| wr3, WSL2 | E2, SIGKILL of supervisor 666 at 16:37:54.154Z | count=7: the supervised command and M1 to M6 alive in the orphaned cgroup. The fresh writer, pid 685, wrote cgroup.kill, which read `populated 0` after 0.43 ms, and the listing 10 s later read count=0 |
602	| xr1, Windows | E1, `TerminateJobObject`, `ActiveProcesses` 0 after 0.31 ms | count=1: the supervisor only. M1 to M4 and M6 ended |
603	| xr2, Windows | E2 with kill-on-job-close (`LimitFlags=0x2000`), `taskkill /F /PID 36852` | count=0 from a fresh process: M1 to M4 and M6 ended |
604	| xr3, Windows | E2 without kill-on-job-close (`LimitFlags=0x0000`), `taskkill /F /PID 35400` | M1 to M4 and M6 alive at +12 s and at +22 s. Fresh processes 26888 and 12844 got `NULL` and error 2 from `OpenJobObjectW`. The driver then ended the survivors |
605	
606	Before each ending, every member was alive and in the spike's job (Windows) or its process group,
607	session and cgroup (macOS and Linux), as each run's `BEFORE ENDING` listing shows.
608	
609	## The table
610	
611	Each cell gives the state at least 10 s after the ending, and the run whose listing shows it.
612	Windows has two E2 rows, because the card runs E2 there twice.
613	
614	| Member | Ending | macOS (laptop) | Linux (WSL2) | Windows |
615	|---|---|---|---|---|
616	| M1 | E1 | ended (m1, mr1) | ended (w1, w7, wr1) | ended (x2, x1, xr1) |
617	| M2 | E1 | ended (m1, mr1) | ended (w1, w7, wr1) | ended (x2, x1, xr1) |
618	| M3 | E1 | **alive** (m1, mr1) | ended (w1, w7, wr1) | ended (x2, x1, xr1) |
619	| M4 | E1 | ended (m1, mr1) | ended (w1, w7, wr1) | ended (x2, x1, xr1) |
620	| M5 | E1 | ended (m1, mr1) | ended (w1, w7, wr1) | not applicable (`TerminateProcess` cannot be ignored) |
621	| M6 | E1 | **alive** (m1, mr1) | ended (w1, w7, wr1) | ended (x2, x1, xr1) |
622	| M1 | E1c | ended (m2, mr2) | ended (w2, wr2) | not run (the card runs E1c on macOS and Linux) |
623	| M2 | E1c | ended (m2, mr2) | ended (w2, wr2) | not run (the same) |
624	| M3 | E1c | ended (m2, mr2, by the census) | ended (w2, wr2, by the census) | not run (the same) |
625	| M4 | E1c | ended (m2, mr2) | ended (w2, wr2) | not run (the same) |
626	| M5 | E1c | ended (m2, mr2) | ended (w2, wr2) | not applicable |
627	| M6 | E1c | **alive** (m2, mr2) | **alive** (w2, wr2) | not run (the same) |
628	| M1 | E2 (Windows: kill-on-job-close set) | **alive** (m3, mr3) | **alive** (w5, wr3) | ended (x3, xr2) |
629	| M2 | E2 (the same) | **alive** (m3, mr3) | **alive** (w5, wr3) | ended (x3, xr2) |
630	| M3 | E2 (the same) | **alive** (m3, mr3) | **alive** (w5, wr3) | ended (x3, xr2) |
631	| M4 | E2 (the same) | **alive** (m3, mr3) | **alive** (w5, wr3) | ended (x3, xr2) |
632	| M5 | E2 (the same) | **alive** (m3, mr3) | **alive** (w5, wr3) | not applicable |
633	| M6 | E2 (the same) | **alive** (m3, mr3) | **alive** (w5, wr3) | ended (x3, xr2) |
634	| M1 | E2, kill-on-job-close not set | not applicable (a Windows job limit) | not applicable (the same) | **alive** (x4, xr3) |
635	| M2 | E2, the same | not applicable | not applicable | **alive** (x4, xr3) |
636	| M3 | E2, the same | not applicable | not applicable | **alive** (x4, xr3) |
637	| M4 | E2, the same | not applicable | not applicable | **alive** (x4, xr3) |
638	| M5 | E2, the same | not applicable | not applicable | not applicable |
639	| M6 | E2, the same | not applicable | not applicable | **alive** (x4, xr3) |
640	
641	After E2 on Linux, a fresh cgroup.kill writer ended every member (w5, wr3). After E2 without
642	kill-on-job-close on Windows, a fresh process could not reach the job (x4, xr3).
643	
644	## Recommendations
645	
646	### Linux under WSL2: a delegated cgroup per dispatch, ended by cgroup.kill
647	
648	The next card should do these things:
649	
650	- Obtain a delegated cgroup from the systemd user manager, with `systemd-run --user -p Delegate=yes`
651	  or an equivalent D-Bus call.
652	- Give each dispatch a child cgroup in it, and place the dispatch's command there before the command
653	  starts anything.
654	- End the dispatch by writing cgroup.kill, and treat the cgroup as ended when cgroup.events
655	  reads `populated 0` or the cgroup is gone.
656	- Record the cgroup's path durably, so that the next start can write cgroup.kill to the orphan.
657	  That is the cgroup analogue of `R-STATE-10`, which w5's fresh writer showed works.
658	
659	This closed every escape the fixture tried, including M6, which today's route left alive (w2). It
660	ended M5 without a `SIGTERM` stage, and it took 0.88 to 1.66 ms. It needed no root.
661	
662	The next card must not assume the following:
663	
664	- That the systemd user manager exists. Here systemd was pid 1, and `user@1000.service` was running
665	  while a login session was open. Neither was tested without a session, nor with WSL's systemd
666	  turned off.
667	- That systemd's default `KillMode` is harmless. It sends `SIGTERM`, which M5 survived (w6).
668	- That a cgroup holds what an agent hands to another manager. A process the agent starts through
669	  `systemd-run --user`, or through D-Bus activation, lands in another unit. That was not measured.
670	  Moving a member out deliberately is outside this card.
671	- That WSL keeps a cgroup across WSL stopping. The brief says WSL stops when idle, and a cgroup does
672	  not survive that.
673	

### 5. 2026-11-22T02:54:56.616Z -> 2026-11-22T02:54:56.642Z  host=laptop  tool=Read
{
 "file_path": "/Users/cjwilliams/.claude/projects/-Users-cjwilliams-GitHub-rigger/17bfb854-c7ed-40ce-af2d-5578b0a83820/tool-results/bstwcou59.txt",
 "offset": 674,
 "limit": 340
}
--- result ---
674	**What would reverse it:**
675	
676	- A target host without the systemd user manager, or with it but without a delegated subtree.
677	- A kernel without cgroup.kill.
678	- An agent workload shown to escape the dispatch's cgroup through a route Rigger cannot close.
679	
680	In the first two cases, the fallback is today's route plus the gap at M6, or a route that needs
681	root.
682	
683	### Native Windows: a Job Object per dispatch, with kill-on-job-close
684	
685	The next card should do these things:
686	
687	- Create one job per dispatch from the supervisor's own process, through koffi or an equivalent
688	  in-process FFI.
689	- Set `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, and set no breakaway limit.
690	- Assign the dispatch's root before it starts children. Node's `spawn` offers no suspended start,
691	  so this needs a gate like the fixture's, or `CreateProcessW` with `CREATE_SUSPENDED` through the
692	  FFI.
693	- End the dispatch with `TerminateJobObject`.
694	
695	Kill-on-job-close then ends the whole tree when the supervisor is killed outright (x3). That is
696	stronger than macOS and Linux, where E2 leaves the tree for the next start.
697	
698	The next card must not assume the following:
699	
700	- That a non-elevated supervisor can do the same. Only an elevated one was measured.
701	- That a job without kill-on-job-close can be reached after the supervisor dies. Its name goes with
702	  the last handle (x4), so a "next start ends the remains" design, as `R-STATE-10` has, does not
703	  carry over without a second handle holder. That was not measured.
704	- That the outer job a host puts Rigger in is benign. Under SSH, the session's job ended every member
705	  on disconnect, which is a containment Rigger did not choose.
706	
707	**What would reverse it:**
708	
709	- A non-elevated supervisor that cannot create or assign the job.
710	- An agent tool that starts processes with `CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB` under a job that allows it.
711	- Resume across a supervisor restart (the Temporal track) turning out to need dispatches that
712	  outlive the supervisor, because kill-on-job-close forbids exactly that.
713	
714	## What this would change, as a proposal
715	
716	This section is a proposal for the PM and the architect. The report changes none of these.
717	
718	| Document | What the recommendation would change |
719	|---|---|
720	| `D13` | Rule 1 makes macOS v0's only host and bars Windows-specific code before the WSL2 spike reports. A Linux or Windows host with its own containment backend is the deferred row "A native Windows host, with its own containment backend" |
721	| `R-STATE-17` | Containment is defined as a process group plus a census of the dispatch's directory. A cgroup or a job would be a containment unit that also holds M6, which the definition leaves outside |
722	| `R-STATE-9`, `R-STATE-10` and `R-STATE-11` | The killed-outright path: the next start ends each recorded process group, and must confirm that the group is still Rigger's. On Linux the recorded thing would be a cgroup path. On Windows, kill-on-job-close would end the tree at once, and nothing would be left for the next start |
723	| `R-STATE-19` and `R-STATE-26` | What Rigger records when it cannot read a group. A cgroup's cgroup.procs and a job's process list are different reads |
724	| `ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Failure model" | L0's containment paragraph: "A process that leaves its group is outside this containment" |
725	| `src/substrate/process.mjs` (`runCommand`) | The code the paragraph describes. This is a pointer for the architect, not a requirement |

1004	## Questions this leaves open, and what would settle them
1005	
1006	| Open question | What would settle it |
1007	|---|---|
1008	| Can a non-elevated Windows supervisor create and assign a job? | Run `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture-win/` from a standard user's SSH session. That needs a non-admin account on the Windows machine, which is a host-configuration change for the owner to approve |
1009	| Does the systemd user manager, and so the delegated cgroup, exist without an open login session? | Rerun w1 with every session closed and `loginctl enable-linger` unset. Enabling linger would be a configuration change |
1010	| Does a second handle holder keep an orphaned Windows job reachable without kill-on-job-close? | One run with a holder process that opens the named job and outlives the supervisor |
1011	| Does a real agent session stay in its cgroup or job? | That is outside this card, by its scope edges |

// ABOUTME: Card #1430 Windows fixture root (the supervised command): node fixture-win.cjs fx1430-<run>-root; waits for a line on stdin, then starts M1-M4 and M6.
'use strict';
const { spawn } = require('node:child_process');
const path = require('node:path');
const run = process.argv[2].replace(/^fx1430-|-root$/g, '');
const member = path.join(__dirname, 'member-win.cjs');
const tag = (m) => `fx1430-${run}-${m}`;
process.stdin.once('data', () => {
  process.stdin.destroy();
  // M1: a direct child; it starts M2, a grandchild.
  spawn(process.execPath, [member, tag('M1'), 'M1'], { stdio: 'ignore' });
  // M3: a descendant started detached, in a new process group (libuv: DETACHED_PROCESS | CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP).
  spawn(process.execPath, [member, tag('M3'), 'leaf'], { stdio: 'ignore', detached: true }).unref();
  // M4: a grandchild whose parent (cmd.exe running `start /b`) exits at once.
  spawn('cmd.exe', [`/d /c start "" /b "${process.execPath}" "${member}" ${tag('M4')} leaf`], { stdio: 'ignore', windowsVerbatimArguments: true });
  // M6: a descendant started detached that changes its working directory to the drive root.
  spawn(process.execPath, [member, tag('M6'), 'M6'], { stdio: 'ignore', detached: true }).unref();
  setTimeout(() => {}, 300000);
});

// ABOUTME: Card #1430 Windows supervisor: creates a named Job Object (kill-on-job-close per mode), starts the fixture root
// detached as Rigger does, assigns it to the job before it starts members, lists, then terminates the job (e1) or waits to be killed (e2k, e2n).
// Usage: node supervisor-win.cjs <e1|e2k|e2n> fx1430-<run>-sup <dispatch-dir>
'use strict';
const { spawn } = require('node:child_process');
const path = require('node:path');
const w = require('./win32.cjs');
const [mode, suptag, dispatch] = process.argv.slice(2);
const run = suptag.replace(/^fx1430-|-sup$/g, '');
const log = (...a) => console.log(new Date().toISOString(), ...a);
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
log('supervisor pid', process.pid, 'mode', mode, 'run', run);
const r = [0]; const ok = w.W.IsProcessInJob(w.W.GetCurrentProcess(), null, r);
log(`IsProcessInJob(GetCurrentProcess(), NULL) before creating a job: returned ${ok}, Result=${r[0] ? 'TRUE (already in a job)' : 'FALSE'}`);
const job = w.W.CreateJobObjectW(null, w.jobName(run));
log('CreateJobObjectW name', w.jobName(run), job ? 'ok' : `failed ${w.W.GetLastError()}`);
if (mode !== 'e2n') log('SetInformationJobObject(JobObjectExtendedLimitInformation, KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE):', w.setKillOnClose(job) ? 'ok' : `failed ${w.W.GetLastError()}`);
log('job limits:', w.limits(job));
const child = spawn(process.execPath, [path.join(__dirname, 'fixture-win.cjs'), `fx1430-${run}-root`], { cwd: dispatch, detached: true, stdio: ['pipe', 'ignore', 'ignore'] });
const h = w.W.OpenProcess(0x1fffff, 0, child.pid);
log('fixture root pid', child.pid, 'AssignProcessToJobObject:', w.W.AssignProcessToJobObject(job, h) ? 'ok' : `failed ${w.W.GetLastError()}`);
w.W.CloseHandle(h);
child.stdin.write('\n');
(async () => {
  await sleep(5000);
  log('BEFORE ENDING'); console.log(w.list(run, job));
  log('job process id list (JobObjectBasicProcessIdList):', JSON.stringify(w.names(w.pidsIn(job))), 'accounting:', JSON.stringify(w.active(job)));
  if (mode !== 'e1') { log('waiting to be killed outright'); await sleep(600000); return; }
  const t0 = process.hrtime.bigint();
  log('TerminateJobObject:', w.W.TerminateJobObject(job, 1) ? 'ok' : `failed ${w.W.GetLastError()}`);
  let ms = null;
  while (Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - t0) / 1e6 < 10000) { if (w.active(job).active === 0) { ms = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - t0) / 1e6; break; } await sleep(1); }
  log(ms === null ? 'job still had an active process 10 s after TerminateJobObject' : `job ActiveProcesses reached 0 after ${ms.toFixed(2)} ms`);
  await sleep(12000);
  log('AFTER ENDING (+12 s)'); console.log(w.list(run, job));
  log('job accounting:', JSON.stringify(w.active(job)));
  process.exit(0);
})();

  if (mode === 'e1') { await new Promise((r) => sup.on('exit', r)); }
  else {
    while (!/waiting to be killed/.test(fs.readFileSync(L, 'utf8'))) await sleep(200);

    console.log(new Date().toISOString(), `taskkill /F /PID ${sup.pid}:`, (k.stdout + k.stderr).trim());

  }
  console.log('---- supervisor log'); console.log(fs.readFileSync(L, 'utf8'));

=== fixture-win/win32.cjs
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 Windows helpers: koffi bindings to kernel32/ntdll Job Object and process calls, and the listing.
'use strict';
const koffi = require('koffi');
const { spawnSync } = require('node:child_process');
const k = koffi.load('kernel32.dll');
const nt = koffi.load('ntdll.dll');
const W = {
  CreateJobObjectW: k.func('void* __stdcall CreateJobObjectW(void*, str16)'),
  OpenJobObjectW: k.func('void* __stdcall OpenJobObjectW(uint32, int, str16)'),
  SetInformationJobObject: k.func('int __stdcall SetInformationJobObject(void*, int, void*, uint32)'),
  QueryInformationJobObject: k.func('int __stdcall QueryInformationJobObject(void*, int, void*, uint32, void*)'),
  AssignProcessToJobObject: k.func('int __stdcall AssignProcessToJobObject(void*, void*)'),
  TerminateJobObject: k.func('int __stdcall TerminateJobObject(void*, uint32)'),
  IsProcessInJob: k.func('int __stdcall IsProcessInJob(void*, void*, _Out_ int*)'),
  OpenProcess: k.func('void* __stdcall OpenProcess(uint32, int, uint32)'),
  CloseHandle: k.func('int __stdcall CloseHandle(void*)'),
  GetCurrentProcess: k.func('void* __stdcall GetCurrentProcess()'),
  GetLastError: k.func('uint32 __stdcall GetLastError()'),
  ReadProcessMemory: k.func('int __stdcall ReadProcessMemory(void*, uint64, void*, uint64, void*)'),
  NtQueryInformationProcess: nt.func('int32 __stdcall NtQueryInformationProcess(void*, int, void*, uint32, void*)'),
};
const KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE = 0x2000;
const jobName = (run) => `Local\\fx1430-${run}`;

function limits(job) {
  const b = Buffer.alloc(144);
  if (!W.QueryInformationJobObject(job, 9, b, 144, null)) return `QueryInformationJobObject(9) failed, error ${W.GetLastError()}`;
  const f = b.readUInt32LE(16);
  return `JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION.BasicLimitInformation.LimitFlags=0x${f.toString(16).padStart(4, '0')} KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE=${(f & KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE) ? 'set' : 'not set'}`;
}
function setKillOnClose(job) {
  const b = Buffer.alloc(144); b.writeUInt32LE(KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE, 16);
  return W.SetInformationJobObject(job, 9, b, 144);
}
function active(job) {
  const b = Buffer.alloc(48);
  if (!W.QueryInformationJobObject(job, 1, b, 48, null)) return null;
  return { total: b.readUInt32LE(36), active: b.readUInt32LE(40), terminated: b.readUInt32LE(44) };
}
function pidsIn(job) {
  const b = Buffer.alloc(8 + 8 * 512);
  if (!W.QueryInformationJobObject(job, 3, b, b.length, null)) return `failed, error ${W.GetLastError()}`;
  const n = b.readUInt32LE(4); const out = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) out.push(Number(b.readBigUInt64LE(8 + 8 * i)));
  return out;
}
function inJob(pid, job) {
  const h = W.OpenProcess(0x1000, 0, pid);
  if (!h) return `OpenProcess error ${W.GetLastError()}`;
  const r = [0]; const ok = W.IsProcessInJob(h, job, r); const e = W.GetLastError(); W.CloseHandle(h);
  return ok ? (r[0] ? 'yes' : 'no') : `IsProcessInJob error ${e}`;
}
function cwdOf(pid) {
  const h = W.OpenProcess(0x0410, 0, pid);
  if (!h) return `?(OpenProcess error ${W.GetLastError()})`;
  try {
    const pbi = Buffer.alloc(48);
    if (W.NtQueryInformationProcess(h, 0, pbi, 48, null) !== 0) return '?(NtQueryInformationProcess)';
    const peb = pbi.readBigUInt64LE(8);
    const p = Buffer.alloc(8); if (!W.ReadProcessMemory(h, peb + 0x20n, p, 8n, null)) return '?(read PEB)';
    const params = p.readBigUInt64LE(0);
    const us = Buffer.alloc(16); if (!W.ReadProcessMemory(h, params + 0x38n, us, 16n, null)) return '?(read params)';
    const len = us.readUInt16LE(0); const buf = us.readBigUInt64LE(8);
    const s = Buffer.alloc(len); if (!W.ReadProcessMemory(h, buf, s, BigInt(len), null)) return '?(read string)';
    return s.toString('utf16le');
  } finally { W.CloseHandle(h); }
}
function processes(run) {
  const ps = `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "CommandLine like '%fx1430-${run}-%'" | Select-Object ProcessId,ParentProcessId,SessionId,CommandLine | ConvertTo-Json -Compress`;
  const out = spawnSync('powershell.exe', ['-NoProfile', '-NonInteractive', '-Command', ps], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout.trim();
  if (!out) return [];
  const j = JSON.parse(out); return (Array.isArray(j) ? j : [j]).filter((p) => !/Get-CimInstance/.test(p.CommandLine));
}
// job: a handle to the spike's job, or null (then only "in any job" is shown).
function list(run, job) {
  const rows = processes(run);
  const lines = [`listing run=${run} at ${new Date().toISOString()} on Windows (Win32_Process + IsProcessInJob + PEB CurrentDirectory)`,
    'PID     PPID    SESS  IN_SPIKE_JOB IN_ANY_JOB CWD | ROLE'];
  for (const r of rows) {
    const role = (r.CommandLine.match(/fx1430-[a-z0-9]+-(\w+)/) || [])[1];
    lines.push(`${String(r.ProcessId).padEnd(7)} ${String(r.ParentProcessId).padEnd(7)} ${String(r.SessionId).padEnd(5)} ${(job ? inJob(r.ProcessId, job) : 'n/a').padEnd(12)} ${inJob(r.ProcessId, null).padEnd(10)} ${cwdOf(r.ProcessId)} | ${role}`);
  }
  lines.push(`count=${rows.length}`);
  return lines.join('\n');
}
// Image name and parent of each pid, for annotating a job's process id list.
function names(pids) {
  if (!Array.isArray(pids) || !pids.length) return pids;
  const ps = `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "${pids.map((p) => `ProcessId=${p}`).join(' OR ')}" | Select-Object ProcessId,ParentProcessId,Name | ConvertTo-Json -Compress`;
  const out = spawnSync('powershell.exe', ['-NoProfile', '-NonInteractive', '-Command', ps], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout.trim();
  const j = out ? [].concat(JSON.parse(out)) : [];
  return pids.map((p) => { const r = j.find((x) => x.ProcessId === p); return r ? `${p}:${r.Name}(ppid ${r.ParentProcessId})` : `${p}:gone`; });
}
module.exports = { names, W, KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE, jobName, limits, setKillOnClose, active, pidsIn, inJob, cwdOf, list };

**Hosts, and whose custody each is under.**

- **The WSL2 machine and the Windows machine are the primary hosts.** Heavy or long runs go there. The spike reaches each over SSH from the laptop ([relay](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1429#issuecomment-7014074437), answer 4), at these hosts:
  - the WSL2 machine: `wsl-hostpc` (`user@10.0.0.29`, port 2222, a Windows port proxy into WSL's sshd; until that sshd answers, WSL is reached through the Windows host with `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e …`);
  - the Windows machine: `hostpc` (`user@10.0.0.29`, port 2223, Windows OpenSSH).
- **The laptop** (macOS) is enrolled under M8's custody rule ([M8-U45](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1293#issuecomment-6980322109), [M8-U58](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1293#issuecomment-6988027619)). It takes light macOS runs only, and none of these:
  - reading, writing or running anything in `~/GitHub/emend-engine`, `~/GitHub/emend-engine-worktrees`, `~/GitHub/emend` or `~/rigger-builds/`, on Emend's board (project 13), or in the williacj/emend repository;
  - a LaunchAgent with the `com.williacj.rigger` prefix, or anything in `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.williacj.rigger*.plist` or `~/Library/Logs/rigger/`;
  - a Rigger run from a checkout, or one against Emend (`R-SAFE-5`);
  - a git GUI, or an IDE that fetches by itself;
  - a long CPU-heavy job during an M8 canary or window period, which rigger-ae announces.
- **The Mac mini** is under the M8 coordinator's custody and is offline. Before any use, the spike tells rigger-ae on this card and waits for rigger-ae to relay the M8 coordinator's OK. Every command there runs through `~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/coordinator-tools/suite-slot.sh <tag> -- …`. The spike never touches the `judge-*` or `codex-*` clones there.

**Rules on how it runs.**

- **Where:** throwaway work lives in a directory named `<card>-spike-engineer`, where `<card>` is this issue's number: under the gitignored `spikes/` directory on a host with a Rigger checkout, and under a scratch directory of the spike's own making on any other host. It never merges (`AGENTS.md`, "When you write code"). Nothing runs Rigger from a checkout (`R-SAFE-5`).
- **Transcript:** the spike keeps a full transcript of every command it runs, each with its UTC time and host, and discloses every write it did not plan, with its reason (owner ruling [P32](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/642#issuecomment-6089559859)).
- **Stated intent:** before its first install or host-configuration change on any host, the spike posts a comment on this card listing each one it intends, with the host. A configuration change is anything outside its own directories that persists: a package install, a firewall rule, a WSL or systemd setting, a registry key.
- **Evidence:** every file the acceptance cites by path, except where an item says otherwise, is committed in the report's pull request under `docs/spikes/<report>/`, beside the report, so a judge opens it at the judged head. The transcript is among them.
- **Sibling spikes share the WSL2 machine.** The three Temporal-track spikes may run there at once. Before a WSL restart, a `.wslconfig` or `/etc/wsl.conf` change, or any other step that ends processes it did not start, a spike posts the step on all three cards and waits for each sibling spike, or rigger-ae, to answer that none of its recorded processes would be ended. #1429's Temporal server uses the default ports, 7233 and 8233. #1431's uses 7243 for gRPC and 8243 for the UI.
- **Processes:** the spike records the pid of every process it starts, and ends only those, or processes descended from them (`AGENTS.md`, "End only recorded processes").
- **Claude Code:** any live `claude` run uses a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` the spike made, never the owner's `~/.claude`.
- **M8 and the live board:** the spike writes nothing to the live Rigger board (project 6), to any card in the M8 milestone, or to any branch but this card's.
- **Temporal:** the local development server (`temporal server start-dev`) is the server for this track. Its docs say it is not intended for production use; this spike measures behaviour, not production fitness.

**Pre-dispatch attack** (architect, under `.claude/skills/acceptance/` "Have it attacked before dispatch": items depend on a system outside Rigger's code). Every citation was opened on `origin/main` at 280ec1bf. The card asks for exact timed commands, full output for failed runs, and every pid recorded at its start. Its items 16–23 under "Acceptance" make each of those ruleable. The findings follow.

1. **The integrity label can be read from the wrong process.** Items 4 and 5 accept "the mandatory label of the supervisor's own process **or of the session that started it**". A filtered or other-account token, which is exactly what this card has to obtain, is a property of the launched process, and the parent shell's label does not prove it. #1430's own route ran `whoami /groups` in the session ("The user and elevation"). **Fix:** both items read the label from the supervisor process's own token, during the run.

**Pre-dispatch attack** (architect, under `.claude/skills/acceptance/` "Have it attacked before dispatch": items depend on a system outside Rigger's code). Its items 17–24 under "Acceptance" make each of those ruleable. The findings follow.

1. **It is unclear whether N2 runs under R0, and that is the main cell.** The cases run "for each route that yields a writable delegated cgroup". With no session open, R0 yields none. With a session open, it yields one (#1430, w1). So a maker can read R0 as yielding nothing and skip N2 under R0. Yet the engine's real case is a user who logs out, with linger unset, while a tree runs. **Fix:** name the cells: N1 and N3 under each route that yields a cgroup with no session open; N2 under each route that yields one with a session open, R0 included.
2. **Something other than `cgroup.kill` can end the tree.** A member may die before the write because the user manager stopped on the last logout, WSL stopped while idle (#1430 notes WSL stops when idle, "Recommendations", Linux), or systemd's `KillMode` sent `SIGTERM`, which M5 survives but others do not (w6). Item 8 reads survival only 10 s after the ending, so it credits `cgroup.kill` with those deaths. **Fix:** for every run, add a listing taken immediately before the ending that shows each member alive. 'yes' : 'no') : `error ${W.GetLastError()}`; }\nfunction limits(job) {\n  const b = Buffer.alloc(144);\n  if (!W.QueryInformationJobObject(job, 9, b, 144, null)) return `QueryInformationJobObject(9) failed, error ${W.GetLastError()}`;\n  const f = b.readUInt32LE(16);\n  return `LimitFlags=0x${f.toString(16).padStart(4, '0')} KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE=${(f & KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE) ?   log('agent exits');
})();

### 17. 2026-11-22T03:01:17.668Z -> 2026-11-22T03:01:17.848Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write runner and sweep scripts"}
cd /Users/cjwilliams/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1460-spike-engineer/spikes/1460-spike-engineer/win && cat > runner.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1460 Windows runner: node runner.cjs <r1|r2> <k1|k2> <kojc 1|0> <run>; inside one SSH session, starts the
// supervisor (and for R1 the handle holder), kills per case with taskkill /F, lists, runs the fresh process, lists again
// at least 10 s later, prints every log, then ends any recorded survivor of the run.
'use strict';
const fs = require('node:fs'); const path = require('node:path');
const w = require('./w.cjs');
const [route, kcase, kojc, run] = process.argv.slice(2);
const log = (...a) => console.log(w.iso(), ...a);
const by = `runner ${process.pid}`;
log(`runner pid ${process.pid}: route ${route} case ${kcase} kojc ${kojc} run ${run}; record:`, JSON.stringify(w.recordSelf('runner', run)));
log('runner session chain:', w.chain());
log('runner job report:', w.jobReport('runner'));
const D = path.join(w.ROOT, `d-${run}`); fs.mkdirSync(D, { recursive: true });
const SL = path.join(w.ROOT, `sup-${run}.log`); const HL = path.join(w.ROOT, `holder-${run}.log`);
const waitFor = async (f, re, ms) => { const t = Date.now(); while (Date.now() - t < ms) { if (fs.existsSync(f) && re.test(fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8'))) return true; await w.sleep(100); } return false; };
const kill = async (e) => {
  log(`kill: ${e.role} pid ${e.pid} (recorded created ${e.created}) alive before: ${w.aliveRecorded(e)}`);
  const r = await w.runRecorded('taskkill.exe', ['/F', '/PID', String(e.pid)], 'taskkill', run, by);
  log(`taskkill.exe /F /PID ${e.pid} exited ${r.code}: ${r.out.trim()}`);
};
(async () => {
  const sfd = fs.openSync(SL, 'w');
  const sup = w.spawnRecorded(process.execPath, [path.join(__dirname, 'supervisor.cjs'), run, kojc, D], { detached: true, stdio: ['ignore', sfd, sfd], windowsHide: true }, 'supervisor', run, by);
  sup.unref();
  log('supervisor READY:', await waitFor(SL, /READY/, 30000));
  let holder = null;
  if (route === 'r1') {
    const hfd = fs.openSync(HL, 'w');
    const hc = w.spawnRecorded(process.execPath, [path.join(__dirname, 'holder.cjs'), run], { detached: true, stdio: ['ignore', hfd, hfd], windowsHide: true }, 'holder', run, by);
    hc.unref();
    log('holder HOLDING:', await waitFor(HL, /HOLDING/, 30000));
    holder = w.readRecord().find((e) => e.run === run && e.role === 'holder' && e.pid === hc.pid);
  }
  const supE = w.readRecord().find((e) => e.run === run && e.role === 'supervisor' && e.pid === sup.pid);
  log('---- the kill');
  await kill(supE);
  if (kcase === 'k2' && holder) await kill(holder);
  await w.sleep(3000);
  log(`supervisor alive: ${w.aliveRecorded(supE)}` + (holder ? `; holder alive: ${w.aliveRecorded(holder)}` : ''));
  console.log(w.listing(run, 'AFTER THE KILL, BEFORE THE FRESH PROCESS ACTED'));
  log('---- the fresh process');
  const f = await w.runRecorded(process.execPath, [path.join(__dirname, `fresh-${route}.cjs`), run], 'fresh', run, by);
  log(`fresh process ${f.pid} exited ${f.code}; its output:`); console.log(f.out.trimEnd());
  log('fresh process done; waiting 12 s');
  await w.sleep(12000);
  if (holder) log(`holder alive: ${w.aliveRecorded(holder)}`);
  console.log(w.listing(run, 'AT LEAST 10 S AFTER THE FRESH PROCESS ACTED'));
  log('runner session chain (still open):', w.chain());
  console.log('---- supervisor log'); console.log(fs.readFileSync(SL, 'utf8').trimEnd());
  if (route === 'r1') { console.log('---- holder log'); console.log(fs.readFileSync(HL, 'utf8').trimEnd()); }
  log('---- cleanup: end any recorded process of this run that is still alive');
  let n = 0;
  for (const e of w.readRecord().filter((x) => x.run === run && x.role !== 'runner' && x.createdFt)) {
    if (w.aliveRecorded(e) !== 'yes') continue;
    const h = w.W.OpenProcess(0x0001 | w.PQLI, 0, e.pid); const ft = h ? w.createdOfHandle(h) : null;
    if (h && ft !== null && ft.toString() === e.createdFt) { log(`cleanup: TerminateProcess(${e.role} ${e.pid}) ->`, w.W.TerminateProcess(h, 1) ? 'ok' : `0, GetLastError=${w.W.GetLastError()}`); n++; }
    if (h) w.W.CloseHandle(h);
  }
  log(`cleanup ended ${n} processes`);
  await w.sleep(2000);
  console.log(w.listing(run, 'AFTER CLEANUP'));
  log('runner done');
})();
EOF
cat > sweep.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1460 Windows final sweep: node sweep.cjs; lists every process on the host with pid and creation time
// (Win32_Process.CreationDate) and reports any that matches a record entry on both pid and creation time.
'use strict';
const { spawnSync } = require('node:child_process');
const w = require('./w.cjs');
const ps = "Get-CimInstance Win32_Process | ForEach-Object { '{0}|{1}|{2}' -f $_.ProcessId, $_.CreationDate.ToUniversalTime().ToString('yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm:ss.ffffffZ'), $_.Name }";
const r = spawnSync('powershell.exe', ['-NoProfile', '-NonInteractive', '-Command', ps], { encoding: 'utf8' });
console.log(`${w.iso()} sweep pid ${process.pid}; powershell pid ${r.pid} exit ${r.status}`);
const rows = r.stdout.trim().split(/\r?\n/).filter(Boolean).map((l) => { const [pid, c, name] = l.split('|'); return { pid: Number(pid), c, name }; });
const rec = w.readRecord();
console.log('PID     CREATED (UTC, microseconds)        NAME');
for (const x of rows) console.log(`${String(x.pid).padEnd(7)} ${x.c.padEnd(34)} ${x.name}`);
console.log(`processes listed: ${rows.length}; record entries: ${rec.length}`);
const us = (s) => s && s.replace(/^(.*\.\d{6})\d*Z$/, '$1Z');
const match = rows.filter((x) => rec.some((e) => e.pid === x.pid && us(e.created) === x.c));
const pidOnly = rows.filter((x) => rec.some((e) => e.pid === x.pid) && !match.includes(x));
console.log(`matching a record entry on both pid and creation time: ${match.length ? JSON.stringify(match) : 'none'}`);
console.log(`matching a record entry on pid alone (reused pids, creation time differs): ${pidOnly.length ? pidOnly.map((x) => `${x.pid} ${x.c} ${x.name}`).join('; ') : 'none'}`);
EOF

$paths = @("$env:LOCALAPPDATA\npm-cache", "$env:APPDATA\npm", "$env:APPDATA\npm-cache", "$env:USERPROFILE\.npmrc", "$env:USERPROFILE\.node_repl_history", "$env:LOCALAPPDATA\Temp")

EOF

### 38. 2026-11-22T03:06:29.051Z -> 2026-11-22T03:06:29.208Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Locate this session's record and the prior session-log generator"}

/Users/cjwilliams/.claude/projects/-Users-cjwilliams-GitHub-rigger/17bfb854-c7ed-40ce-af2d-5578b0a83820/subagents/agent-a3c31636d68810716.jsonl
# ABOUTME: Card #1430: turns the spike engineer's retained Claude Code session record (JSON lines) into a plain-text log of every tool
# call, with its UTC issue and completion times, the host it acted on, its exact input and its full result, redacting one account name.
import json, re, sys
src, dst = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
uses, results, order = {}, {}, []
for line in open(src):
    d = json.loads(line)
    if d.get('type') not in ('assistant', 'user') or not d.get('timestamp'):
        continue
    c = d.get('message', {}).get('content')
    if not isinstance(c, list):
        continue
    for x in c:
        if x.get('type') == 'tool_use' and x['id'] not in uses:
            uses[x['id']] = (d['timestamp'], x['name'], x['input']); order.append(x['id'])
        if x.get('type') == 'tool_result':
            body = x.get('content')
            if isinstance(body, list):
                body = '\n'.join(b.get('text', '[non-text block]') if isinstance(b, dict) else str(b) for b in body)
            results[x['tool_use_id']] = (d['timestamp'], bool(x.get('is_error')), body if body is not None else '')
def host(name, inp):
    if name != 'Bash':
        return 'laptop'
    cmd = inp.get('command', '')
    if 'wsl.exe' in cmd or 'wsl-hostpc' in cmd: return 'laptop -> hostpc -> WSL'
    if 'hostpc' in cmd: return 'laptop -> hostpc'
    return 'laptop'
def redact(s):
    s = re.sub(r'MicrosoftAccount\\\S+(\s+)User(\s+)S-1-11-96-[0-9-]+', lambda m: 'MicrosoftAccount\\[redacted: account name]' + m.group(1) + 'User' + m.group(2) + 'S-1-11-96-[redacted]', s)
    s = re.sub(r'[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[name withheld]\.com', '[redacted: account name]', s)
    return s
out = ['ABOUTME: The tool calls in the #1430 spike engineer\'s retained Claude Code session record, with UTC times, host, input and the result text the record holds.', '',
       'Generated by gen-session-log.py from the session record of subagent a9a3ffa429348b341. Times are UTC: the first is when the call was issued, the second when its result returned.',
       '"host" is read from the command text: laptop unless the command reaches hostpc over ssh, and WSL when it runs wsl.exe. A command that ran in the background shows its launch result here; its output file is named in the result.',
       'Redactions: one Microsoft account name and its SID, wherever whoami /groups output appears. Nothing else is removed. A call that returned an error is marked ERROR.',
       'Scope: only calls the record holds up to the time it was generated. A result the harness saved to a file holds only a preview here, and a background command holds only its launch notice. The report, under "The transcript", lists those calls and what the log lacks.', '']
for n, i in enumerate(order, 1):
    t0, name, inp = uses[i]

ABOUTME: What a journal entry is, and how the file names in this directory order the entries.

# Journal

An entry records something learned or something that failed, dated, in as few lines as that
takes. It binds nothing — a rule that came out of an entry is written where rules live, and the
entry says what taught us. `AGENTS.md` holds when an entry is committed.

Each entry is its own file here, and `AGENTS.md`, "Version control", holds the name form. Its
parts are the date and time the entry was written, the card that produced it, and a short form
of the entry's heading.

Two properties follow from that form:

- A card owns every name it writes, so two cards working at once never write one file and never
  collide over one.
- The date and time are fixed width, so the names sort into reading order without being opened,
  oldest first ascending and newest first descending, and no line inside a file carries it.

# #607 — enumerate an outside system's states, and attack the acceptance before dispatch

**The budget left no room, so the card stopped until O90.** At `e9a5eca`, `npm run
budget:instructions` measured the live pool at 13,997 words against 14,000. The draft measured
14,482 and exited 1, over by 482. The maker escalated rather than trim other rules. The owner's
O90 raised the budget to 14,600, and the one-line change to `ARCHITECTURE.md` rides in this card,
which is why the owner judges and merges it.

**The worked example is #586 and #599, because the missed cell is one row of a table.** #586 named
one sequence of reads after the kill: every read fails. The sequence where one read answers and
the rest fail is the row next to it, and all three of #589's judges found it. The other misses
the card cites (#555, #605) would have needed a longer table to show the same point.

**Spec-style is unchanged.** Its rules govern form only, and say they never license a change to
what a ratified clause means. Whether a row is bounded is a question about its meaning. A sixth
form rule would also change the "five rules" that the spec-style lint's tests read.

**The PM's prompt is unchanged.** The review before dispatch binds whoever files a card, not the
PM alone, and the PM already loads the acceptance skill before writing an acceptance. Restating
it in `pm.md` would narrow it to the PM, which is what agent-style's one test forbids.

**The templates move with the skills.** `test/init.test.mjs` holds `.claude/` byte for byte
to what `init` writes from `templates/claude/`, and the pre-commit hook refused the skill edits
until the templates matched. The card's "Lands in" did not name them; the pull request does.

**One test pinned the old budget.** `test/instruction-budget.test.mjs` asserts the ratified figure,
so O90 changes it. The maker posted the rows on #607 and changed them only once granted.

### 42. 2026-11-22T03:06:51.042Z -> 2026-11-22T03:06:51.376Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Read the 1430 journal entry"}

--- result ---
ABOUTME: Journal for #1430: whether cgroup v2 under WSL2 and Job Objects on Windows end an agent's whole
process tree, against the macOS baseline and today's route.

# #1430 — Whole-tree containment on Linux under WSL2 and on Windows

## What ran

On 2026-11-20 a throwaway fixture ran on three hosts: macOS on the laptop, Ubuntu 26.04 under WSL2,
and native Windows. The fixture was six sleeping members, two of which left the session and one of
which moved to `/`. Each host ran it under each ending the card names. The code stayed in the
gitignored `spikes/`. Its source, every listing and the transcript are in
[the report](../spikes/whole-tree-containment.md).

## What we learned

- **A cgroup cost nothing on this WSL2 host.** systemd was pid 1, and the user manager handed an
  unprivileged user a writable cgroup through `systemd-run --user -p Delegate=yes`. `cgroup.kill`
  ended every member in 1.66 ms. An orphaned cgroup stayed reachable by its path after the
  supervisor died.
- **The shell `wsl.exe -e` starts is outside systemd's tree.** It sits in `/non-systemd`, owned by
  root. A delegated cgroup needs the user manager to start the process, or a scope to move it.
- **A Windows job's name dies with its last handle, not with the job.** Without kill-on-job-close,
  the members outlived the supervisor in a job that no fresh process could open.
  `OpenJobObjectW` failed with error 2.
- **Today's route misses a process that leaves the session and the dispatch directory together.**
  M6 survived the group kill and the census on macOS and on Linux alike.
- **The SSH session is itself a job on Windows,** and it ended every survivor on disconnect.
  Windows readings taken over SSH must finish inside one session.
- **systemd's default `KillMode` sends `SIGTERM`,** which the member that ignores it survived.

## What failed

- **`cmd.exe` ate the arguments of the first WSL run,** and broke one inspection command at its
  pipes. After that, every WSL command went in as a script on stdin.
- **A polling loop waited for `populated 0` from a cgroup that systemd had removed,** and spun until
  I ended it. WSL's pid counter advanced by about 101,000 while it ran (2377 to 103714). The rerun's loop also stops when the
  cgroup is gone.
- **`supervisor.js` would not load,** because the repository's `package.json` sets
  `"type": "module"`. Throwaway CommonJS in `spikes/` needs the `.cjs` extension.
- **A non-elevated Windows run could not be made.** `runas /trustlevel` failed over SSH, so every
  Windows reading is from an elevated supervisor.

## Round 2

- **"Every process" means every pid, and that has to be designed in before the run.**
  - Round 1 recorded only the long-lived processes, and the judge was right that synchronous
    helpers and M4's parent are processes too.
  - Round 2 recorded each spawn in Node, made the fixture write its own pids, and ran a WMI
    process-start watcher on Windows.
  - Even so, the measuring sessions themselves cannot be fully recorded, and the report names what
    is left.
- **A watcher must outlive the events it is meant to catch.** Check A stopped the Windows watcher
  before WMI had delivered check A's own start events.
- **A command log is cheapest to produce from the session record.** Round 1's hand-kept
  appendix could only give time windows. The retained tool-call record gives exact inputs, times
  and outputs.

### 46. 2026-11-22T03:08:08.784Z -> 2026-11-22T03:08:09.003Z  host=laptop -> hostpc  tool=Bash  {"description": "Check record times precede the next command on Windows"}
